Financial Report Source 10

Document 001: 10 questions for China in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/10 questions for China in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 24
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 has been a year of slowing growth and increasing
divergence within China's economy. We saw the gap between
external and domestic demand, as well as between the new
economy and the old economy, widen signiﬁcantly. Here, we
look ahead to the key questions facing China in 2027
Lynn Song
Beijing, China
1) Will China’s growth target be lowered
again?
Despite a growing emphasis on the quality of growth rather than
just the pace, China’s annual economic target-setting exercise in

March remains one of the year's most important events. The GDP
growth target and ﬁscal quotas tend to determine the level of
policy support needed in the year ahead.
China very rarely misses its growth target. Since 1991, it has fallen
signiﬁcantly short of target only once – after the widespread
pandemic lockdowns in 2022.

### Page 2

In 2026, lowering the growth target from “around 5%” to 4.5-5.0%
set the stage for slower activity and less urgency for stimulus than
in previous years. We expect the target to be achieved, but growth
will likely be toward the low end of the range.
The key question for 2027 is whether we’ll see the growth target
soften further. China’s economy has seen growing imbalances over
the past year. Next year’s growth will rely heavily on external
demand and new-economy drivers. Uncertainty about whether

external demand and the AI-driven tech capex boom can continue
could aﬀect overall growth.
We expect a slight softening of the growth target to “around 4.5%”.
This would allow for decent policy ﬂexibility while keeping the long-
term challenges in mind. Our calculations show China will need to
grow at an average of around 4% per year to achieve the 2030
(RMB170tr) and 2035 (RMB207tr) targets set by Premier Li Qiang
and President Xi Jinping, respectively. A target of “around 4.5%”

gives China some buﬀer room if growth slows more signiﬁcantly
going forward.
Accordingly, we expect growth to come in at 4.5% year-on-year in
2027, broadly in line with our 4.6% forecast for 2026. This will
maintain China’s role as a key driver of global growth. China is
expected to contribute somewhere between a ﬁfth and a quarter of
global growth over the next ﬁve years.
China's growth target may be adjusted to
"around 4.5%" in 2027

### Page 3

2) Will external demand and tech drive
growth again?
We observed two major K-shaped trends in 2026: the divergence
between strong external demand and weak domestic demand, and
the split between the tech-led new economy and the old economy.
We expect export growth to moderate but remain relatively strong
next year, with high-single-digit increases in exports. While things
are likely to normalise a bit after an extraordinarily strong year in
2026, external demand will still remain one of China's key growth

engines.
First, strong export performance in 2026 will create a challenging
base eﬀect to follow in 2027. The run-up in energy and technology
prices that underpinned growth is also unlikely to be repeated,
reducing the scope for a similar boost next year.
Second, uncertainty could return in 2027 after what has been a
relatively calm year in terms of trade developments following a
roller-coaster 2025.
The China-US trade truce at the time of writing is set to expire in

January 2027. While our base case is a further extension of this
agreement, risks of re-escalation can’t be ruled out, particularly
as China’s trade surplus widened 12% YoY to $233.8bn through
the ﬁrst three quarters of 2026. The ‘blue wave’ election
outcome in the US could lead to gridlock and prompt US
President Donald Trump to focus on areas where he can
exercise executive power, such as trade policy.

### Page 4

Possible trade tensions with the EU are also worth considering.
ASEAN has overtaken the US as China’s largest export
destination. The EU remains second, representing 14-15% of
China’s total exports. China’s trade surplus with the EU surged
26.1% YoY to $277.4bn in the ﬁrst three quarters of 2026. After
trade talks in October, China and the EU agreed to a 16-point
plan to improve trade ties. The plan includes a cap on exports
of hybrid vehicles into the EU at 2025 levels. Almost

immediately, calls emerged to extend the quotas to other
industries. Almost immediately after, there were additional
calls to extend these quotas to other industries. While we
believe there’s plenty of room for win-win cooperation, there’s
also risk of trade friction increasing in the year ahead.
On the tech side, 2026 marked an acceleration of the capex cycle
and AI boom. Hi-tech investment was one of the few areas that saw
positive investment growth in 2026, and tech-related exports and

industrial production also outperformed on the year.
However, we also saw several notable market pullbacks along with
rising fears of an AI bubble bursting. One of the biggest questions
for not just China but the global economy at large is whether
impressive tech growth continues into 2027.
At this juncture, the AI-driven investment boom looks likely to
continue into 2027, with many companies still reporting order
books full through 2027. We think the current engines of growth will

moderate but continue to drive growth into 2027. To achieve stable
growth, other parts of the economy might need to pick up the
slack. Our next question deals with whether domestic demand can
step up to the plate.
Technology products and export
diversiﬁcation continue to support external
demand

### Page 5

3) Can domestic demand carry more of the
load?
Anyone following Chinese policymakers' messaging will have
noticed a recurring theme in recent years: the need to increase the
role of domestic demand in driving growth.
China’s 15th Five-Year Plan places building a strong domestic
market among its top priorities, alongside industrial
modernisation, tech self-reliance, and the digital economy. Yet
while the latter three have been obvious recipients of policy backing

and resources, the push to strengthen domestic demand has often
appeared less tangible. These categories are clearly policy
favourites and receive more resources.
However, 2026 was one of the weakest years for domestic demand
in recent history. Over the ﬁrst three quarters, China’s retail sales
grew just 1.1% YoY ytd.
In our view, several factors played a major role in weak
consumption growth in 2026:
Trade-in programme turned from tailwind into headwind.

China’s ﬂagship policy to boost consumption was the trade-in
programme. It front-loaded consumption by oﬀering consumer
subsidies for targeted products across categories including
autos, household appliances, furniture, and communication
devices. The programme's scale fell slightly in 2026, which
along with previous front-loading clearly dragged retail sales
growth, with household appliance, furniture, and auto sales
down year on year.
Consumer conﬁdence ﬂatlined after a modest recovery in

2025. Conﬁdence peaked in February 2026, but pulled back

### Page 6

afterwards. Low wage growth, combined with a negative
wealth eﬀect from falling equity and property prices, was the
main culprit for soft conﬁdence. Weak conﬁdence continued to
reduce discretionary consumption categories for most of the
year.
The peak and subsequent drop of gold prices dragged gold &
jewellery sales. Gold prices peaked in January. The subsequent
bust signiﬁcantly reduced retail interest in buying gold.
Heading into 2027, we expect a modest recovery in consumption,

with potential upside for a larger recovery.
Drag from trade-in programme should be smaller. The
pullback we saw from the household appliances, furniture, and
auto categories should be smaller in 2027. It’s too early to
expect signiﬁcant replacement demand for appliances and
furniture, but auto sales could begin to see some replacement
demand from the ﬁrst wave of EV sales. This could reduce the
category's drag. However, given the strength of communication

appliance sales in 2026, this category is ripe for a 2027
pullback.
Policy support for domestic demand could pick up, especially
if the external demand picture worsens. While it seems
reasonable to expect resources to remain concentrated toward
tech thanks to the AI race, supporting domestic demand could
become more important if we see signs of the external demand
engine slowing in 2027. Longer term, increasing domestic
demand is necessary to achieve more sustainable growth.

The big wildcard is how consumer conﬁdence fares next year. The
main factors will likely be wage growth and asset price
performance. We’ve seen some encouraging signs that property
prices are beginning to bottom out in tier 1 and a few tier 2 cities.
This could start to translate into a conﬁdence recovery. While wage
growth has remained slow amid a weak job market, a recovery of
corporate proﬁts could potentially translate into a recovery if the
trend holds.

### Page 7

Weak consumer conﬁdence remains the
main constraint on domestic demand
4) Has China successfully escaped
deﬂation?
China’s struggle with deﬂation in the past few years triggered
signiﬁcant debate about whether China was headed down the
same path as Japan’s Lost Decades. Wealth destruction from the
property market bust, aggressive price competition, and poor
domestic demand contributed to three straight years of near-zero
inﬂation. It also threatened to put China into a negative feedback

loop of falling prices and proﬁtability, lower wage growth, and
weaker consumption and investment.
However, in 2026 we ﬁnally saw a more convincing uptick in
inﬂation, ending the year above 0% for the ﬁrst time since 2022.
We think the worst of the deﬂationary pressure may be behind us.
Looking ahead to 2027, several factors are likely to continue
shaping the inﬂation outlook.
Food and housing have been the two biggest drags on inﬂation
over the past year, together accounting for more than half of

China's CPI basket. The drag from food should gradually fade as
pork prices eventually stabilise. Pork has started to see sequential
increases starting in the second half of 2026. Abundant domestic
supplies of fresh vegetables and fruits should continue thanks to
greenhouse expansion, but likely won't repeat the same
deﬂationary impact next year.

### Page 8

Rent may continue to drag on inﬂation until we see a broader
bottom in the property market. Rent has been at -0.6% YoY since
April 2026.
This year’s primary inﬂation driver was energy prices, as fallout
from the Iran war hit the global economy. Energy costs are a
wildcard in 2027. Our house forecasts Brent averaging $81/bbl in
2027, which should keep inﬂation elevated after higher prices in
2026.
Tech prices, by contrast, surged in 2026 as the AI boom drove

heavy demand and price hikes across many tech supply chains.
This was most evident in the communication appliances
component of China's CPI basket, where prices rose 6.3% YoY in the
ﬁrst three quarters.
Furthermore, China’s PPI inﬂation surged in 2026. Producer prices
rose amid higher raw material and other input prices. Companies
have been reluctant to pass higher costs on to consumers amid
subdued conﬁdence. But if input costs remain elevated into next

year, maintaining that restraint may become increasingly diﬃcult.
Overall, we expect China’s CPI inﬂation to move a little higher next
year, to 1.1% YoY. This level is a sweet spot for China, staying a
comfortable distance from deﬂation while avoiding the high
inﬂation pressure many other economies are currently facing. At
this level, we don’t expect inﬂation to constrain monetary easing if
needed.
Inﬂation has turned higher, but the recovery
remains uneven

### Page 9

5) Will the drag from the property market
ﬁnally fade?
Barring an astounding end-of-year turnaround, property prices will
have fallen for a ﬁfth consecutive year by the time 2026 wraps up.
China's property downturn has evolved considerably over the past
few years. Fears of a catastrophic systemic crisis have largely
faded, and developer defaults have become less frequent. Yet home
prices still haven’t found a clear ﬂoor, and the pessimism unleashed

by the housing slump remains deeply embedded in household
sentiment. The property market remains a major overhang on the
economy.
Building sales and real estate ﬁxed-asset investment continued to
see steep declines, down -13.9% YoY and -20.6%, respectively.
The indirect impact of the property bust is harder to measure, but
it’s widespread and arguably even more signiﬁcant.
Consumer conﬁdence has been weak since the property
downturn began, and the recovery has been slow and uneven

since.
Adjacent industries have been impacted. Retail sales of
furniture (-4.7%), household appliance sales (-4.9%), and
building & decoration materials (-9.7%) are in contraction this
year. Industrial production data shows signiﬁcant weakness in
property-related categories such as cement (-8.6%), ﬂat glass
(-7.0%), and steel (-2.0%) through the third quarter.
Land sales traditionally have been a major source of local
government revenue. This dried up as demand plummeted

amid the property bust. Local-government revenue from
transfers of state-owned land-use rights fell from CNY8.7tn in
2021 to CNY4.15tn in 2025, and looks to have fallen further in
2026. The consequence of this is that local governments are
also tightening their belts in terms of spending.
Falling home and rental prices are contributing to deﬂationary
pressure.
Is the drag from the property sector going to fade in 2027?

### Page 10

In our view, two things need to happen before we see a property
sector turnaround. First, we need to see signs that price
stabilisation is becoming more entrenched. This year has delivered
the most encouraging evidence since the property downturn
began, with prices in tier-one cities broadly stabilising or rising for
much of the year. If that trend persists and spreads to more tier-two
and tier-three cities, it could mark the bottom of the current cycle.

For now, the 70-city average property price index has yet to
establish a bottom.
Second, property inventories remain elevated, reaching a new
record high in early 2026 before dropping later in the year. Until
these return to more normal levels, it's diﬃcult to expect signiﬁcant
new property investment.
Policy support for the property market has expanded modestly in
2026, including a mortgage subsidy for ﬁrst-time buyers, further
loosening of purchase restrictions across the country, and

continuing with urban renewal projects.
We also saw some structural reforms. These include tightening
restrictions on the property pre-sale model, such as requiring
developers to reach a certain stage of completion before pre-sales
and requiring funds to enter a supervised account. These steps
make the operating environment more challenging for developers
but should boost buyer conﬁdence.
With further support for the property market, we think we could see

a smaller drag from the sector in 2027. If price stabilisation
continues, it would be a big step, removing a major weight on
household and investment conﬁdence. That said, with inventories
still high and developers limiting new projects, real estate
investment will likely remain a headwind next year.
High inventories and falling prices continue
to weigh on the property market

### Page 11

6) What can we expect from policymakers
in 2027?
With the 2026 growth target lowered and attention consumed by
the AI race, it is perhaps unsurprising that policy support for the
broader economy has been underwhelming this year. We expect a
generally supportive policy environment for the year ahead, with
the possibility for more aggressive policy rollout to bolster the
domestic economy if we see trade tensions pick back up next year.
On ﬁscal policy, we expect the key ﬁscal targets to remain fairly

stable in 2027.
The ﬁscal deﬁcit target is likely to remain around 4% of GDP for
a third straight year. Adjustments to this target would be
primarily for signalling purposes rather than to actively
constrain policy.
Our base case for bond issuance targets is that they remain
little changed, around RMB 4.4tn for special local government
bonds and RMB 1.3tn for ultra-long-term bonds. Given the
limited appetite for using approved quotas in 2026 amid the

drop in investment, it's possible that targets could be lowered.
Our base case is for targets to stay high to increase ﬂexibility.
On monetary policy, we expect the People’s Bank of China to buck
global trends and maintain an easing bias. Inﬂation remains well
below target, the labour market remains fairly soft, growth has
been decelerating, and the CNY has been strengthening. Conditions
are mostly suitable for further easing, with the key exception being

bank net interest margins are already at historical lows, and
policymakers have thus far preferred targeted easing instead.
Below are our key calls for the PBOC next year:

### Page 12

We look for a single 10bp rate cut to the benchmark rate. The
PBOC has resisted broad-based rate cuts, mindful of low
banking-sector margins and the fact that many major central
banks have remained in hiking mode.
Targeted measures to support selected industries look likely
to continue. Re-lending programmes, interest rate subsidies,
and risk-sharing facilities may remain the main tools for
allocating money to areas of need.
PBOC benchmark rate shift will improve monetary policy

transmission. The PBOC formalised a shift away from the 7-
day reverse repo rate to the overnight reverse repo rate starting
2 November 2026. We expect this shift to improve monetary
policy transmission, enable more precise day-to-day liquidity
management, and move China’s framework closer to global
peers.
At the sector level, we expect policy priorities in 2027 to remain
closely aligned with those laid out in the Five-Year Plan, with
industrial modernisation and technological self-reliance continuing

to attract the lion's share of attention and resources.
Given recent communications, we may also see more concerted
eﬀorts to build out domestic demand. An October National
Development and Reform Commission (NDRC) news conference
oﬀered some food for thought, promising to “strengthen inclusive
policies that reach consumers directly” alongside existing strategies
to expand and upgrade goods such as new energy vehicles and
smart products. One limitation of recent consumption-support

measures is that, while trade-in programmes have successfully
brought forward spending, they have done little to lift household
conﬁdence. The next step may be to recalibrate policy to make
consumers feel genuinely better oﬀ.
Support for the property market should also continue to spread the
initial recovery seen in the core cities nationwide.
Policy will remain supportive, but broad
stimulus is unlikely

### Page 13

7) Will CNY two-way moves return after
2026's gains?
We set an initial forecast band for the USDCNY in 2027 of 6.40-6.80,
and our 2027 year-end forecast set at 6.50.
2026 marked another strong year for the CNY, as the appreciation
momentum started in 2025 and continued into 2026 despite what
has largely been a strong dollar backdrop. Generally, appreciation
has been stronger than what most market participants had
expected at the start of the year.

As the numbers above imply, our base case has appreciation
continuing. Our key reasons are listed below:
We continue to hear a very strong consensus among market
participants for a moderate pace of appreciation to continue.
While this raises risks of a sharper reversal, it also remains
diﬃcult to bet against.
Exporters continue to sell into upticks of the USDCNY, creating a
convincing environment for CNY appreciation to continue.
Considering the foreign currency left abroad over the past few

years, and incoming foreign currency receivables with China’s
trade surplus set to hit a new record high in 2026 and remain
strong in 2027, the stage is set for further CNY appreciation.
PBOC has signalled it has neither the need nor intention to use
currency depreciation to gain advantages in trade. This
suggests that there won't be a push to depreciate the currency
even if the current trade boom cools oﬀ.
CNY strength has thus far been without the support of equity

market inﬂows. Chinese markets have been an underperformer.
Your privacy is important

### Page 14

But if there's some mean reversion or a bull market reversal,
this could further support CNY strength.
However, we don't think 2027 will see a repeat of this relatively
simple straight line appreciation from April 2025 to the time of
writing. Two-way ﬂuctuation looks likely to return, and the recent
CNY appreciation bias in markets raises the risk for a stronger
reversal, even if the PBOC acts to limit excessive volatility.
US-China yield spreads remain near-record highs. This

correlation weakened in 2026 as investors became worried
about missing out on further appreciation, and as the spread
narrowed to start the year. However, a hawkish shift as the Iran
war broke out has widened the yield spread to new highs. This
should eventually tempt investors.
PBOC could step in if the appreciation trend goes too far. In an
October policy statement, the PBOC warned against one-sided
and procyclical behaviour in the FX market, reﬂecting concern

that market participants can reinforce an exchange-rate move
through a herding eﬀect. In early November, the PBOC raised
the foreign-exchange deposit reserve requirement from 4% to
6%. It was widely seen as a warning against excessive yuan
appreciation and one-sided speculative positioning,
discouraging this behaviour by draining foreign-currency
liquidity from the banking system.
Outward direct investment looks to be closing a year with an
uncharacteristic year-on-year decline after seeing double-digit

growth for 5 of the past 6 years. A recovery of ODI demand
could add selling pressure to the CNY.
CNY appreciation should continue, but with
greater two-way volatility

### Page 15

8) Are CGBs the biggest asymmetric risk to
Chinese markets?
China’s government bond yields bucked global trends in 2026,
falling despite a surge in yields elsewhere in the world amid rising
inﬂation and debt.
The falling yields have been a double-edged sword. On one hand,
low yields have allowed the government to issue debt cheaply, an
important beneﬁt as China brings shadow-banking debt onto
oﬃcial balance sheets, and as debt levels remain high. However, the

low yields have also become a symbol of weak investor conﬁdence
and a factor cited by those arguing that China is seeing entrenched
pessimism and on a path of “Japanisation.”
China’s government bond yields have generally trended downward
since 2020, with pockets of volatility throughout. Several key
factors have contributed to this long bull market:
Investor risk appetite remains abnormally soft. Although we
would argue that China exited “peak pessimism” in 2024,

many market participants still maintain very conservative
asset allocations. Equity and property markets continue to
underperform, and demand for risk-free assets remains huge.
Limited credit demand has led to rising bank holdings of CGBs.
Bank holdings of CGBs have nearly quadrupled over the past
decade and stood at RMB 78.6tn at the end of 3Q26.
Many remain trapped in a deﬂationary mindset. China has
taken steps to move out of deﬂation in 2026, thanks to higher

energy prices that boosted core inﬂation. Yet the deﬂationary
mindset persists for many, contributing to a willingness to
accept such low yields.

### Page 16

Tighter restrictions on outbound investment may be
preventing investors from investing in higher-yielding bonds
abroad. China’s yields are now signiﬁcantly lower than most
major economies, but domestic investors still face limitations
on how much they can move abroad to capitalise on this
spread. Capital controls remain a major factor in how yields
can stay so low.
We think there’s a solid case for bond yields to turn around in 2027.
A slight normalisation of risk appetite would be suﬃcient to

cause a major shift. A return to a more balanced asset
allocation would be a catalyst for risk asset rallies and a bond
market pullback. Banks are investing in bonds instead of
lending thanks to unusually weak investment appetite. A
reversion to the mean would be the biggest catalyst for a bond
selloﬀ.
China’s long-term growth and inﬂation outlook don’t match
the current yields. Current yields imply a deﬂationary,
stagnant environment, while most economists expect

moderating but still solid growth and low but positive inﬂation.
Time will tell which side is right, but bond yields suggest
markets are pricing in a signiﬁcantly more downbeat outlook
than what the data is showing.
Bond issuance is expected to remain high, and debt levels
remain elevated. As we discussed earlier in this report, China’s
ﬁscal policy stance is likely to remain accommodative. Another
year of high bond issuance should add some supply pressure

on the CGB market.
Higher global yields make Chinese yields even less attractive
by comparison. China’s bond yields are now among the lowest
of major economies. Capital controls will limit how far investors
can chase higher yields, but an asset allocation shift from
domestic to global bonds could still play a role.
Policymakers may start to worry about the implications of
low long-end yields, and push back if the bull market
continues. Recall that back in 2024, the PBOC had repeatedly

intervened to keep the 10-year CGB yield above 2%. While
these eﬀorts were discontinued, market rumours at the end of

October suggested that policymakers had asked Chinese
ﬁnancial institutions not to add duration in the bond market.
We look for 10-year CGB yields to rise to 1.9% by the end of 2027.
The key risks to this outlook are further worsening sentiment, a
return of deﬂation, more aggressive-than-expected rate cuts, or
tighter capital controls that keep domestic money locked up in the
CGB market.
Heavy bond issuance has been absorbed by
rising bank demand

9) How is the AI boom impacting China?
AI has become one of China’s more important growth engines,
supporting exports, manufacturing, investment and productivity. At
the same time, the heavy focus on the AI race means it is also a
major driver of the K-shaped divergence in China. It's likely to
continue to widen the gap between industries and companies.
China’s strong trade growth on both the export and import sides
were in large part tied to AI-related demand. Over 6,000 Chinese AI

ﬁrms across AI infrastructure, framework, and applications
contributed to heavy demand. We saw categories such as
automatic data processing machines, semiconductors, and general
computer & electronics with high double-digit and even triple-digit
export and import growth.
A similar pattern was observed in China’s manufacturing industry.
Technology-intensive manufacturing signiﬁcantly outperformed
the broader industrial in 2026, led by electronic equipment and

semiconductors. Chinese companies across a broad range of

### Page 18

industries are integrating AI into quality control, from precision
electronics manufacturing to chilli oil. AI is already playing a role in
China’s industrial upgrading process.
Hi-tech investment has been the lone bright spot in an otherwise
down year for investment, with hi-tech FAI up 4.9% YoY but
headline FAI down -7.2% YoY through 3Q26. Despite an equity
market selloﬀ as investors start to question valuations, the AI capex
cycle itself looks to have further room to run. Spending by major

Chinese tech companies has doubled from their 2022 levels, with
continued investment in computing capacity, data centres and AI
infrastructure. China’s grid investment data has been volatile but
has generally shown strong growth for the last few years as well.
The impact of AI on the labour market is a hot topic globally. In
China, aggregate employment data have yet to show a broad AI-
driven shock, but ﬁrm-level evidence already points to higher

output per worker and lower labour requirements in selected tasks.
The initial impact is therefore more likely to appear through slower
hiring and job redesign than immediate mass layoﬀs.
We think two factors could make this adjustment particularly
important for China in 2027:
AI adoption could move faster in China. China’s AI strategy
places greater emphasis on industrial deployment and
applications, while public trust and workplace usage are
relatively high compared to global peers. An ageing population

also gives ﬁrms a stronger incentive to automate in response
to further labour shortages. Faster AI adoption has shown
signs of supporting labour productivity growth. It has
accelerated for two straight years to reach 6.3% in 2025 after
dropping as low as 4.4% in 2023. But it could also accelerate
the displacement of substitutable roles and increase downward
occupational mobility.
Entry-level employment will face the greatest pressure. China
is expected to have 12.7 million university graduates in 2026,

480,000 more than in 2025, adding to an already competitive
youth labour market. At the same time, AI is increasingly able
Your privacy is important

### Page 19

to perform the information intensive and administrative work
which traditionally assigned to junior employees. Firms may
therefore reduce entry-level hiring without cutting their existing
workforce, narrowing the career ladder through which young
workers acquire skills and experience.
The government has rolled out policies to address the potential
impact of AI on employment, focusing on displacement risk
assessment, digital skills training, worker transitions and the

creation of new occupations. We expect these measures to
moderate, but not prevent, pressure on junior and routine white-
collar employment. The key issue for next year is whether new
service-sector and AI-complementary jobs can be created quickly
enough to absorb aﬀected workers.
Overall, we expect AI to support productivity growth and industrial
upgrading in 2027, but the gains will remain uneven. If commercial
returns disappoint the market’s high expectations, a reassessment

of the global AI cycle could weaken related demand. This could
leave China with stronger productive capacity but divergence across
industries and labour markets.
The other scenario worth considering is if we see a widespread AI
bust. Increasingly, market participants may increasingly shift their
focus to commercial returns of AI, where there is arguably still a lot
of froth in the markets. Given China’s heavy investment in the AI
race, and its prominent role in the outperforming areas of the

economy, a reversal of this trend would naturally have signiﬁcant
implications. We’ve already seen a signiﬁcant selloﬀ in equities,
which does not help the already soft conﬁdence. A moderate
pullback may not necessarily be bad news, as it could allow for
more balanced resource allocation elsewhere, but a crash would
pose larger risks to the growth outlook.
China's AI capex cycle accelerated sharply
after 2023

10) How is China faring in its Great
Transition?
Our key long-term call back when ﬁrst joining ING’s research
department in 2024 was that China was not in a Great Decline, but

Your privacy is important

Targeting Cookies

### Page 21

a Great Transition. At that time, discourse on China was arguably at
its most pessimistic, with many investors deeming China
uninvestable and amid worries over systemic risks. China’s
reopening after the pandemic fell short of expectations, and many
worried its outlook was doomed.
Almost three years later, China is in a signiﬁcantly diﬀerent place.
While a cloud of pessimism still hangs over the economy at large,
the narrative has become a lot more balanced. Within China’s

widening K-shaped trajectories, pockets of extremely strong growth
remain.
We outlined four main directions for China’s Great Transition:
Developing domestic demand and consumption as a growth
driver
Moving up the value-added ladder
Strengthening the green transition
Advancing tech and digitalisation
We’ve since seen progress across all four fronts, though the speed
has been uneven.
Developing domestic demand is arguably the most disappointing
of the four key transitions so far. Chinese households’ willingness

to save has hit a new high, and weak conﬁdence continues to
weigh on this eﬀort. Retail sales slowed to a post-pandemic low in
2026. Critics of China often cite weak domestic demand as a main
factor behind growing trade imbalances.
Policymakers have said all the right things. But for now, eﬀorts to
buoy domestic demand seem to have taken a backseat to
industrial modernisation and the tech race. We do, however, see
progress in the right direction. For example, trade-in programmes

helped front-load consumption, and policymakers have vowed to
“strengthen inclusive policies that reach consumers directly.”
China has already made great progress in moving up the value-
added ladder. The evolution of China’s industrial production and
exports shows its manufacturing backbone moving from the low
end toward the high end.

### Page 22

Exports show that the share of machinery and transport equipment
in total exports rose from 48.6% in 2023 to 56.0% in 3Q26.
Traditionally low-end miscellaneous manufacturing, including
clothing and footwear, has dropped from 22.8% in 2023 to 18.6%
in 3Q26.
This transition has major global implications, prompting discussion
of a so-called “China Shock 2.0” as the economy produces
increasingly high-end products. China's auto industry has rapidly
risen to global prominence, capitalising on the accelerating

worldwide adoption of electric vehicles.
China’s green transition has continued its great progress. China
has undergone a remarkable green transition over the past decade-
plus. Various indicators point to continued success in this green
transition in recent years. China’s PM 2.5 levels across 168 cities hit
a record low of 15 in July 2026. Renewable and nuclear power
generation as a percentage of total energy production rose from
21.5% at the end of 2023 to 26.2% at the end of 2025.

The green transition remains an important medium- and long-term
goal. As such, it will likely continue to outperform in terms of
growth. In 2025, President Xi pledged to lower emissions by 7-10%
by 2035 while “striving to do better”, building on 2020 comments
aiming to achieve peak carbon by 2030 and reach carbon
neutralisation by 2060.
Heavy investment continues in green industries. China is a leading
player in key sectors such as EVs, solar, and wind power generation.

This shift is clearly aﬀecting the global economy through trade and
investment.
China’s eﬀorts to advance technological development and
digitalisation have exceeded our expectations. Chinese ﬁrms have
proven to be competitive in the AI race. China has taken major
steps to improve self-reliance, including strengthening the domestic
semiconductor industry and developing the robotics industry.
Digitalisation of the economy continues to build on existing

strength. According to China’s National Data Administration, the

core industries of China’s digital economy accounted for over

There’s also a ﬁrm commitment to continue heavy investment in
research and development. China’s 15  Five-Year Plan committed
to setting an R&D expenditure growth target of at least 7% per year
through 2026-2030. R&D spending reached RMB 3.92tn in 2025,

equivalent to 2.8% of GDP.
In conclusion, China has made good but uneven progress in its key
economic transitions over the past few years. Strong progress in the
tech and green transitions, along with China’s aggressive move up
the value-added ladder, laid a foundation for future growth.
However, domestic demand must be strengthened to drive more
sustainable long-term growth. This is particularly true as we see
increasing pushback from trading partners about China’s growing

trade surplus. In China’s “dual circulation” strategy, the internal
circulation side must be strengthened in the coming months and
years.

article.

輝達產品時程恐因領導層變動遞延，台股供應鏈重挫
2026 年 11 月 16 日
一、市場動態
1. 輝達執行長閃辭衝擊延燒，台股重挫逾 2500 點：黃仁勳於 11 月 12 日辭去輝達總裁、
執行長及董事職務，由財務長 Colette Kress 暫代執行長，資深董事 Harvey Jones 出
任首任董事長，市場擔憂產品時程與決策速度出現不確定，加上本周財報即將發布，
加深市場觀望情緒。台股今日受上週五美股半導體重挫拖累開低走低，收最低至
41,040 點、下跌 2635 點或 6.03%。台積電跌 135 元或 5.74%，聯發科跌逾 7%，台

達電、創意及台光電、欣興等 PCB／載板族群觸及跌停，高價 AI 股成為賣壓重心。
2. AI 晶片族群同步降溫，資金由「換股」轉為「降低整體曝險」：輝達換帥後，市場原預
期資金將轉進 AMD、博通及 ASIC 等替代方案，惟上週五美國半導體族群全面回檔，
替代受惠股跌幅甚至更大，顯示投資人擔憂的已非單一公司接班問題，而是 AI 投資循
環的整體風險。
二、下跌原因
1. 領導層更替落地，產品時程與決策速度出現不確定：輝達在此事件之前，約 60 名高階
主管直接向黃仁勳匯報，權力高度集中，接班空窗期決策速度恐下降。即使在其任內，
年更節奏已觸及供應鏈物理極限，Rubin CPX 已移出路線圖，Kyber 機櫃傳延至 2028
年。新任管理層若偏重成本與執行穩定，路線圖可能改為「大世代兩年、中間推出強
化版」，800V HVDC、共同封裝光學（CPO）、 M9 等級銅箔基板等升級導入時點恐再
遞延。
2. 規格升級放緩衝擊「本夢比」，高價零組件股首當其衝：台廠 2027 年成長有相當部分
來自 Vera Rubin 單櫃價值約為 GB300 兩倍，而單櫃價值主要來自規格升級。市場擔

憂高階液冷、HVDC 電源與光互連若由 2027 年遞延至 2028 年放量，供應鏈單價、
毛利率與 EPS 成長預估恐面臨下修；缺乏技術突破支撐的高本益比族群評價修正，今
日 PCB／CCL、散熱及電源族群跌停家數最多，即反映此一疑慮。
3. 生態系融資與出口管制成為新增變數：市場估計輝達每季自由現金流約 500 億至 600
億美元，若過渡期暫緩投資及融資交易，生態系資金供給可能暫時減少 500 億美元以
上；據報 Kress 曾反對為 OpenAI 提供最高 1,050 億美元擔保，新增融資審核恐趨嚴，
依賴輝達融資的 NeoCloud 及其供應商受影響較大。此外，事件涉及晶片流向中國之
指控，美方據報擬將訂定較嚴格出口限制，市場估潛在曝險約占輝達營收一成。
4. 期中選舉後，可能的出口管制收緊以及短線獲利了結需求：此事件涉及晶片流向中國之
指控，出口管制收緊將衝擊整體 AI 晶片；台股上週反彈近 4,000 點，短線獲利了結需
求升高，避險心態未退，放大 AI 權值股賣壓。

三、市場看法
1. 2027 年出貨大致鎖定，衝擊先落在評價而非營收：Vera Rubin 已於 8 月量產出貨、
2027 年放量；輝達對供應商之採購與產能承諾達 2,790 億美元，台積電、HBM 及主
要組裝廠 2027 年產能多已預訂。廣達法說表示迄今未見客戶調整訂單，鴻海亦預期
明年 AI 機櫃出貨高雙位數成長。依 ToAlpha 基本情境推算，輝達 CoWoS 份額相對
原本路徑 2027、2028 年僅分別減少 2 與 4 個百分點，CoWoS 總量僅減約 0.6%與

0.8%，影響主要在 2028 年後的產品節奏。

2. 台股供應鏈由分化轉為齊跌，ASIC 族群抗跌性消失：依曝險分組，10 月 29 日至 11
月 12 日以輝達平台為主者平均下跌 14.0%，GPU 與 ASIC 兩邊都有者下跌 17.0%，以
ASIC 為主者下跌 9.4%，同期加權指數下跌 7.1%。兩邊都有之族群多為前波漲幅大的

規格升級零組件股，跌幅反而最深，顯示市場賣的是「評價」而非「訂單」；今日台股
盤勢延續上周五費半情緒，以 ASIC 為主及兩邊都有之代表股平均重挫逾 8%，以輝達
平台為主者跌約 4.4%，顯示賣壓轉向前波漲多、評價較高的 ASIC 與規格升級股，市
場已由「區分受益者」轉為「全面降低 AI 曝險」，短線錯殺機會浮現。

3. 算力多元化早於本次事件，Google TPU 與 AMD 最直接受惠：根據彭博在本次事件發
生前的統計，市場預估輝達占全球 CoWoS 需求比重將由 2026 年 56%降至 2027 年
45%，AMD 由 9%升至 20%，聯發科由 3%升至 7%，預計本次事件發生後，此趨勢
可能進一步加劇。由於 AMD、博通與雲端業者自研 ASIC 同樣高度依賴台積電先進製
程及台廠組裝與零組件，訂單多在台灣供應鏈內部移轉，台灣在 AMD、Google 供應
鏈同樣扮演重要角色。

4. 歷史經驗顯示 CEO 更替後股價無一致方向，基本面才是中期關鍵：回顧美股大型科技

不另行通知。
資料來源：Bloomberg、公司資料、元大銀行整理；黃底為突發性更替案例
5. 本週三大事件為短線關鍵驗證點：（一）輝達將於美東 11 月 17 日（台灣時間 11 月 18
日凌晨）公布第三季財報，為首度由暫代執行長主持之法說會，焦點在 2028 會計年度
約七成成長指引是否重申、Vera Rubin 放量節奏及新增融資態度；（二）Anthropic 預
計 11 月 18 日定價上市，為檢驗 AI 市場熱度之指標；（三）黃仁勳預計感恩節前赴國
會作證，牽動晶片出口管制法案走向。若財報與指引穩健，市場有望由情緒面回歸基本

面。
四、投資建議
1. 科技股短期審慎、中期仍正向，AI 供應鏈基本面持續具支撐：黃仁勳辭任使輝達面臨
管理層接班、企業治理及政策調查等不確定性，加上 Fed 升息預期及 AI 資料中心監
管疑慮，市場短線波動可能延續。然而，該事件並未直接改變 AI 算力需求，且上週
CoreWeave Q3 財報營收年增 168%、台積電 10 月營收年增 59.7%，均顯示 AI 基礎
建設投資仍具動能。整體對科技股維持短期審慎、但中長期基本面仍在，台灣及日本
等具技術競爭優勢、獲利與現金流穩健的半導體及 AI 供應鏈亦值得留意。惟市場焦點
已由資本支出規模轉向投資報酬與現金流，後續仍須關注輝達財報及管理團隊能否維
持客戶信心。
2. 以股債平衡穿越波動，善用多元資產降低單一事件風險：台股權重高度集中於台積電
與 AI 供應鏈，對單一事件敏感度高。建議將集中度過高的台股科技部位，適度轉向全
球型或多重資產商品，搭配中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債穩定收益；待接班人
選、財報指引與出口管制方向明朗後，再逐步提高風險資產比重。

2317 鴻海精密工業(FOXCONN)

報告日期：2026/11/13 研究員：曾智楷(Kevin Tseng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆，YoY +46% ，毛利率 6.02%，EPS 5.51
元。單季營收、營業利益、稅後淨利均創下歷年單季新高。 
 受惠於 AI 營運持續成長及 ICT 產品進入傳統出貨旺季，公司
預期 4Q26 整體營收季對季與年對年均將呈現強勁成長。AI 伺
服器部分，由於新一代機櫃市佔率較上一代提升，預期第四季
AI 機櫃出貨量將維持高雙位數季增。 
 訂單能見度轉佳，公司上修 2026 年全年四大產品展望，對 2027
年全年營運持審慎樂觀看法，預計整體營收維持雙位數成長，
AI 機櫃則預估明年出貨維持高雙位數成長。

一. 3Q26 營收、獲利續創單季新高，EPS 5.51 元 
鴻海 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆，QoQ +19% ，YoY +46% ，續創歷史新
高，主要受惠於 AI 伺服器呈現強勁成長，其中，第三季 ASIC AI
伺服器出貨量年增 1.5 倍，高於 GPU 約 70%的成長率，帶動整體
AI 機櫃達成高雙位數成長目標，且新一代 AI 機櫃已開始出貨。就
各產品別來看，3Q26 雲端網路產品佔整體營收的 54%；消費智
能產品佔 29%；電腦終端產品佔 12%；元件及其他產品佔 5%，
而獲利方面，3Q26 營業毛利 1815.1 億，QoQ +17%，YoY +39%，
毛利率 6.02%，QoQ -0.1ppts，YoY -0.33ppts，營業利益 1153.5
億，QoQ +22% ，YoY+64%，營利率 3.83%，QoQ +0.07ppts ，
YoY +0.4ppts，3Q26 業外淨利 33.6 億，稅後淨利 775.2 億，QoQ 
+29%，YoY +34%，單季稅後 EPS 5.51 元，創歷年單季新高。

二. AI 機櫃放量、ICT 產品進入旺季，4Q26 營運有望持
續成長 
公司公告 10 月營收 1 兆 3053 億，MoM +12.7%，YoY +45.7%，
今年累計營收 8.97 兆，YoY +40.4% 。展望 4Q26，受惠於 AI 營
運持續成長及 ICT 產品進入傳統出貨旺季，公司預期整體營收季
對季與年對年均將呈現強勁成長。其中，AI 伺服器部分，由於新
一代機櫃市佔率較上一代提升，預期第四季 AI 機櫃出貨量將維持
高雙位數季增。而傳統 ICT 產品，消費智能產品在旺季需求與 ASP
提升下，預期將呈現強勁表現；電腦終端產品則受限於記憶體等
零組件供應吃緊影響，預期將略微季減。預估公司 4Q26 營收 3.59
兆，QoQ +18.7%，YoY +37.9%，毛利率 5.8%，營利率 3.5%，
稅後淨利 811 億元，單季稅後 EPS 5.77 元。 
投資評等：買進

三. 預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 19.08/23.59 元 
展望 2026 年，由於目前訂單能見度較 8 月更佳，公司上調 2026
年全年四大產品展望，整體營運維持強勁成長。預估 2026 全年營
收 11.26 兆，YoY +39%，毛利率 6.0%，營利率 3.7%，稅後淨利
2685 億，全年稅後 EPS 19.08 元。展望 2027 年，公司對明年全
年營運持審慎樂觀看法，預計維持雙位數成長，其中 AI 仍為最主
要的成長動能。受惠於全球算力需求與基礎設施投資，全球主要
CSP 明年持續擴大 AI 資本支出，預期公司雲網產品佔比將在未來
3 到 5 年，從目前的五成以上進一步提高至 60%以上。而 AI 機櫃
部分，預期 2027 年 AI 機櫃出貨維持高雙位數成長，且客戶除了
大型 CSP 客戶之外，明年將加入更多 NeoCloud 客戶，市佔率將
進一步提升。此外，隨 AI 推論需求增加帶動 ASIC 伺服器快速成
長，其高度客製化需求亦有利公司發揮零組件垂直整合優勢以支
撐獲利表現。消費智能產品由於主要客戶產品以高階為主，受記
憶體等供應鏈短缺影響相對有限，再加上新產品開發與規格升級
需求，預計 2027 年整體表現相對穩定。而電腦終端產品，由於記
憶體供應緊張與零組件價格上漲，可能推升終端售價並影響消費
者換機意願，對明年 PC 市場看法較為審慎。預估 2027 全年營收
14.9 兆，YoY +33%，毛 利率 5.4%，營利率 3.3%，稅後淨利 3320
億，全年稅後 EPS 23.59 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 330 元 
考量到 CSP 持續上修 2027 年資本支出展望，AI 伺服器維持強勁
需求，隨著新一代 AI 機櫃推出，有望進一步拓展公司在 AI 伺服
器領域市佔率，帶動公司營收、獲利持續成長。目前公司股價交
易於 2027 年 EPS 約 10~11 倍本益比，評價仍有提升空間，給予
投資建議為買進，目標價 330 元(14x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
鴻海精密工業(FOXCONN)為全球最大 eCMMS(電子化-零元件、
模組機光電垂直整合服務)廠，在電子代工服務領域(EMS)排名全
球第一，市占率超過四成。鴻海近年積極投入「電動車、數位健
康、機器人」三大新興產業以及「人工智慧、半導體、新世代通
訊技術」三項新技術領域，以「3+3」結合作為集團重要的長期發
展策略。在 EMS 板塊，鴻海主要競爭對手為和碩聯合科技、廣達
電腦、緯創資通、仁寶電腦、英業達及立訊精密等。

二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

股價相對大盤走勢

強茂(2481 TT) Buy

車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器拉貨帶動，投資建議 Buy。  
投資建議：展望未來，隨著車用市佔率持續擴大，加上雲端資
料中心與 AI 伺服器新世代平台功率元件拉貨需求攀升，給予 
Buy 的投資建議，目標價 160 元。 
車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器拉貨，3Q26 獲利三率三升：  
強茂 3Q26 營收 43.50 億元（QoQ +10.0%、 YoY +33.0%），主
因車用及 AI 需求帶動功率元件需求轉強；毛利率因產品組合
優化提升至 37.3%（ QoQ +4.1 ppts、 YoY +6.0 ppts）；營業利益 
6.97 億元（QoQ +50.1%、 YoY +152.3%）；稅後淨利 6.49 億
元（QoQ +33.0%、 YoY +114.0%），3Q26 EPS 為 1.70 元。 
產品組合車用比重提升，產能佈局強化東南亞供應： 
YTD 3Q26 終端應用營收比重以車用 35%最高（YOY+2ppts），
車用產品營收較去年同期大幅成長 47.2% 。AI 與雲端運算營收
佔整體營收逾 12%，拉貨將逐步提升。集團擁有三座晶圓廠及
五座後段封測廠，隨著 2026 年下半年越南胡志明市新封測廠加
入營運，進一步強化東南亞供應能力。預估 2026 年營收 
160.48 億元（YoY +22.6%），EPS 為 5.47 元。 
車用市佔率持續擴大，MOSFET 業務加速放量與定價能力提
升：隨著 AI 伺服器機櫃功耗提高，強茂成功切入美系 CSP 的  
ASIC 伺服器與 AI 機櫃電源供應鏈，主攻 Hot-Swap（熱插拔）
與 ORing（冗餘電源管理兩大關鍵應用），推動 AI 佔比朝 15%
以上邁進。車用方面，除了 40V 車規中低壓 MOSFET 持續放
量外，更推出碳化矽（SiC）MOSFET 搶攻日歐美市場。綜上所
述，預估強茂 2027 年營收 192.31 億元（YoY +19.8% ），EPS 
8.41 元。基於車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器新世代平台拉貨需求攀
升，給予 Buy 投資建議，目標價 160 元。

### Page 2

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
在  ESG 永續發展表現上，強茂積極落實環境保護、供應鏈管理與綠色技術
創新三大面向： 
環境永續：持續投入節能減碳與能源轉型，透過製程節能改善及再生能源建
置，2025 年全年節電約 91.1 萬度，減少約 432 公噸 CO₂e 排放；太陽能系
統全年發電量突破 263 萬度，穩健提升綠電使用比重並朝向低碳營運邁進。
責任供應鏈：依循責任商業聯盟(RBA)標準深化供應鏈管理，年度完成 75 家
供應商 RBA 稽核且合格率達 100%，攜手合作夥伴打造具韌性的永續價值
鏈。 
創新與產品發展：持續深耕高效率功率半導體研發，2025 年集團累計有效
專利達 102 件，並維持 90 分以上的客戶滿意度，以高效能半導體解決方案
協助終端客戶提升能源使用效率，共同響應全球淨零碳排趨勢。

公司簡介： 
強茂成立於 1986 年，為台灣功率半導體領導廠商之一，主要從事離散元件
（ Discrete）之研發、製造與銷售，產品涵蓋 MOSFET 、Schottky、Rectifier、
TVS、 Zener、 Bridge Transistor、 ESD Protection 及  SiC 等。公司母公司專
注於核心元件開發，並透過關係企業與子公司進行垂直與水平整合，包含熒
茂光學、凡甲科技、虹冠電子、汎星半導體及新貿科技。強茂在低功率矽整
流器領域位居全球第四大供應商，並持續聚焦車用電子、雲端資料中心與 AI
伺服器等高附加價值應用。 
主要競爭對手分析 
1. 國際一線 IDM 大廠（高階車用與 AI 伺服器主要對手） 
強茂近年積極搶攻歐美車廠與 AI 機櫃市佔，主要在以下領域與國際巨頭
正面交鋒（並受惠於供應鏈去中化或對手產能受限之轉單效應）： 
• 安世半導體（Nexperia）：最直接的車用與小訊號元件競爭對手。安
世在車用小訊號二極體、ESD/TVS 保護元件及中低壓 MOSFET 市
佔率極高；每當安世出現產能吃緊或地緣政治/出口管制干擾時，歐美
Tier 1 車廠往往優先將訂單轉移至強茂。 
• 意法半導體（STMicroelectronics） 、 安森美（onsemi） 、 英飛凌
（ Infineon） ：全球車用與工業功率半導體前三大巨頭，在車規
MOSFET、SiC（碳化矽）及 AI 伺服器電源管理擁有完整生態系。強
茂以高性價比及交期彈性切入其二供（Second Source）體系。 
• 威世（Vishay）、達爾（Diodes Incorporated）、羅姆（ROHM）：
在整流二極體、蕭特基二極體及中低壓 MOSFET 產品線高度重疊，
是強茂在美系與日系客戶端的主要比價與份額競爭者。 
2. 台灣分離式元件同業 
• 台半（5425）：在整流二極體與車用 MOSFET 布局深厚（切入 Tier

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

3 群益投顧 
1 車廠供應鏈），與強茂在車用整流器與 40V/60V 車規 MOSFET 
領域重疊度最高。 
• 朋程（8255）：專注車用發電機二極體（全球市佔第一）及高效能二
極體（LLD/ULLD）、48V MOSFET 與  SiC 模組，在車用動力系統
與強茂具備相似的客戶群。 
• 富鼎（8261）、杰力（5299）、大中（6435）：台灣純 MOSFET 設
計公司（Fabless），主力集中於 PC/NB 、主機板與一般電源供應器，
與強茂在消費性電子及部分電源應用市場競爭。

圖一、強茂營收比重

資料來源：強茂，群益投顧彙整

車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器拉貨，3Q26 獲利三率三升： 
強茂 3Q26 營收 43.50 億元，QoQ+10.0%，YoY+33.0%，主因車用及 AI 需求

### Page 4

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

4 群益投顧 
帶動功率元件需求轉強；毛利率因產品組合優化提升至 37.3% ，
QoQ+4.1ppts，YoY+6.0ppts；營業利益 6.97 億元，QoQ+50.1%，YoY+152.3%，
呈現大幅提升；稅後淨利 6.49 億元，QoQ+32.96%， YoY+113.95%， 3Q26 
EPS 1.70 元，3Q26 財報表現亮眼。 
產品組合車用比重提升，產能佈局強化東南亞供應： 
YTD 3Q26 終端應用營收比重以車用 35%最高，年增 2 ppts，車用產品營收
較去年同期大幅成長 47.2%。 AI 與雲端運算營收佔整體營收逾 12%，受惠
新世代平台規格推進拉貨穩健。集團擁有三座晶圓廠及五座後段封測廠，隨
2026 年下半年越南胡志明市新封測廠加入營運，進一步強化東南亞第三供
應能力，2H26 營運持續增長，4Q26 營運預估與 3Q26 持平，預估 2026 年
營收 160.48 億元(YOY+22.56%)， EPS 為 5.47 元。 
車用市佔率持續擴大，MOSFET 業務加速放量與定價能力提升： 
強茂本身具有歐美車廠供應鏈能力，在安世半導體產能與出貨受阻後，客戶
端為確保供貨安全，加速將小訊號二極體與保護元件（ESD/TVS）訂單轉移
至強茂，使推升其車用營收比重與整體市佔率。隨著 AI 伺服器機櫃功耗
大幅提高，資料中心對供電穩定度與電源轉換效率的要求越發嚴苛。在成功
切入美系 CSP（雲端服務大廠）的 ASIC 伺服器與 AI 機櫃電源供應鏈後，
強茂主攻 Hot-Swap（熱插拔）與 ORing（冗餘電源管理）兩大關鍵應用。這
類高電流供電路徑需要具備「超低導通電阻（Low RDS(on) ）」的中高壓
MOSFET 來降低功耗與發熱，其 ASP 遠高於傳統消費性元件，預估 AI 佔比
可朝 15%以上邁進。 
車用展望方面，目前佔強茂整體營收比重約 35%。隨著電動車與智慧座艙滲
透率提升，車內馬達驅動、電池管理系統（BMS）及車載充電器對 MOSFET 
的用量呈倍數增長。強茂除了既有的 40V 車規中低壓 MOSFET 持續放量
外，更進一步推出碳化矽（SiC）MOSFET 搶攻日本與歐美車用市場，產品
線從小訊號保護元件成功延伸至高毛利的動力與電源核心。綜上所述，預估
強茂 2027 年營收 192.31 億元(YOY+19.8%)， EPS 8.41 元。 
投資建議：展望未來，隨著車用市佔率持續擴大，加上雲端資料中心與 AI 
伺服器新世代平台功率元件拉貨需求攀升，給予 Buy 的投資建議，目標價
160 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
All Ring Tech
Bright 2027 outlook with multiple drivers
Intact 2027 outlook
All Ring's 2027 outlook is robust, supported by three key drivers. 1) We expect TSMC
and ASE to continue accelerating CoWoS capacity expansion, with industry capacity
reaching ~270kwpm by end-2027E, up from ~160kwpm by end-2026. We estimate
TSMC and ASE’s capacity could reach ~180kwpm and ~70kwpm, respectively, by
2027E. 2) Second, with Intel's EMIB-T progressing on track toward volume production

by early 2028, we expect related equipment demand to begin ramping in 2027,
providing a new growth driver for All Ring. 3) CPO’s fiber array unit (FAU) coupling tools
start contributing to All Ring's 2027 revenue, with demand from Nvidia and AMD.
More concrete CPO development in 2027-28
Our industry feedback suggests Nvidia is taking the lead to aggressively push CPO mass
production in 2027 for scale up switch solution. AMD is also engaging with key

equipment suppliers, in preparation for competing CPO solution. Competition among
the equipment vendors is fierce, but we believe All Ring is well positioned in FAU
coupling as a tier 1 supplier, given the superior execution in driving higher tool
throughput versus peers. Considering the meaningful tool demand in 2027-28, we
forecast All Ring to recognize ~100 tools for revenue in 2027E, contributing ~12% of All
Ring's sales next year. Potential upside to our current estimates could come from larger

TAM for faster capacity expansion, All Ring securing higher share / ASP through better
tool performance.
Lower Q3 gross margin due to mix
Q3 EPS came in below LSEG consensus by -10% as gross margin of 48.5% missed
consensus of 54.2%. Lower gross margin could be due to a higher mix of automation
products, which carries a lower margin vs corporate average. For Q4, considering lighter
seasonality and current visibility, we model sales to decline by -28% from Q3 with gross

margin of 53% and EPS of NT$7.57.
Valuation: Maintain Buy and target of $1700
Post Q3 results, we fine-tune 2026 EPS by -4% due to lower gross margin in the quarter,
though our estimates for 2027/28 are largely kept unchanged. Our 2027E/2028E EPS
are 3%/30% ahead of Consensus pre-earnings. We maintain our Buy rating and price
target of $1700 based on 27x average 2027-28E PE.

Company Description
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based supplier of automation equipment for the
semiconductor back-end packaging and passive component manufacturing industries. The
company's products include underfill dispensers, heatsink placement and automated optical
inspection (AOI) tools for advanced packaging equipment, particularly for heterogeneous

integration. Revenue is generated primarily from sales of semiconductor and electronic
manufacturing equipment and related automation solutions, with more than 90% of
revenue derived from Taiwan.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value All Ring based on PE methodology.
Risks for All Ring and the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in

the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech

companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in All Ring
gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in All
Ring losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could

lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to All
Ring, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact All Ring’s
margins for equipment shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

SK하이닉스 (000660)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G는 +11.5%, ASP는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

SK하이닉스 (000660)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

SK하이닉스 (000660)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

ASP 움직임은 메이커별로 
차별화 
HBM4는 예정대로 순항 
중 
HBM 8Hi는 GPU를 더 
팔기 위한 수단 
공장 짓는다고 물량이 
늘어나진 않는다 
26년 주주환원은 100조원 
수준

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

그림 5. SK하이닉스 투자자별 순매수 추이(2010년부터 누계)  그림 6. SK하이닉스 마이크론 밸류에이션 비교

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Document 331: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Allocation to global equities decrease to 52.5% from 
53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

US and Asia ex-Japan would continue to lead in 
earnings growth in 2027

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR
Timeline of select events

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 19 2026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

### Page 4

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.
Stablecoins payment use case is steadily growing

CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer value proposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FX and Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?

market cap here I would say.

INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
